L1782: ## COVID-19
L1783: The Applicant included COVID-19 flags in the dataset. See Section 8.1.2 under Protocol Violations/Deviations where COVID-19 flags were used for minor deviations reported in the clinical trial. In addition, in the ADAE dataset, there was one patient (b)(6) flagged with a COVID-19 related TEAE reported in the Exon 20 Ins prior chemotherapy (n=129) group. The non-serious, Grade 1 COVID-19 related event was reported in a 72 year old male; amivantamab therapy was interrupted until the patient recovered.
L1784: See Protocol Violations/Deviations in Section 8.1.2 of this review for details regarding COVID-19 related protocol deviations, all of which were classified as minor protocol deviations.

L1804: Figure 11 displays Western blot analysis and cell viability graphs for DFCI-127 (P772_H773insPNP) and DFCI-58 (H773_V774insNPH) cells treated with Amivantamab (0, 0.1, 1 mg/mL) compared to IgG1 control.

The cell viability graphs show that Amivantamab dose-dependently reduces cell viability compared to IgG1 control in both cell lines.
L1805: Figure 12 displays immunofluorescence images illustrating amivantamab-induced trogocytosis of H1975 cells incubated with M1 or M2 macrophages. The images are arranged in two main panels, each showing results for H1975 cells incubated with M1 Macrophages (top) and M2 Macrophages (bottom). Within each panel, there are two rows of images corresponding to the presence of the anti-EGFR antibody, either JNJ-372 (top row) or IgG2\alpha (bottom row). The columns represent time points (T=11, T=44, T=77, T=110) after incubation. The macrophages are stained green (Hoechst M1/M2 Macrophages (Cd11b/CD14)), and the H1975 cells are stained red (H1975-NucLight Red). White arrows indicate areas of cell-cell contact, and yellow arrows point to specific features, likely representing trogocytosis events where macrophage components are transferred to the H1975 cells. The images demonstrate that amivantamab (JNJ-372) enhances trogocytosis compared to IgG2\alpha at later time points, particularly in the presence of M1 macrophages.

DFCI-127 (Left): Shows concentration-dependent cytotoxicity for IgG1 control (dashed line), Amivantamab (red line), and Cetuximab (blue line). Amivantamab exhibits the highest cytotoxicity, reaching approximately 35% at 100 \mug/mL. Cetuximab shows moderate cytotoxicity, reaching approximately 20% at 100 \mug/mL. IgG1 control shows minimal cytotoxicity. A significant difference (*) is noted between Amivantamab and Cetuximab cytotoxicity at 100 \mug/mL.
YU-1163 (Middle): Shows concentration-dependent cytotoxicity for IgG1 control (dashed line), Amivantamab (red line), and Cetuximab (blue line). Amivantamab exhibits the highest cytotoxicity, reaching approximately 60% at 100 \mug/mL. Cetuximab shows moderate cytotoxicity, reaching approximately 30% at 100 \mug/mL. IgG1 control shows minimal cytotoxicity. A significant difference (*) is noted between Amivantamab and Cetuximab cytotoxicity at 100 \mug/mL.
Comparison (Right): Bar chart comparing cytotoxicity (%) for DFCI-127 and YU-1163 cell lines across four conditions: IgG1 (light gray), Amivantamab (black), Amivantamab + FcR blocker (red), and IgG1 control (dashed line). For both cell lines, Amivantamab shows the highest cytotoxicity. The addition of an FcR blocker significantly reduces Amivantamab cytotoxicity in both cell lines (indicated by *).
IFN\gamma Secretion (Bottom Row):
DFCI-127 (Left): Bar chart showing IFN\gamma secretion (pg/mL) for Untreated, IgG1, Amivantamab, and Cetuximab conditions. Untreated and IgG1 show low secretion. Amivantamab and Cetuximab show high secretion, with Amivantamab slightly higher than Cetuximab. A significant difference (*) is noted between Amivantamab and Cetuximab secretion.
YU-1163 (Middle): Bar chart showing IFN\gamma secretion (pg/mL) for Untreated, IgG1, Amivantamab, and Cetuximab conditions. Untreated and IgG1 show low secretion. Amivantamab and Cetuximab show high secretion, with Amivantamab slightly higher than Cetuximab. A significant difference (*) is noted between Amivantamab and Cetuximab secretion.
Comparison (Right): Bar chart comparing IFN\gamma secretion (pg/mL) for DFCI-127 and YU-1163 cell lines across Untreated, IgG1, Amivantamab, and Cetuximab conditions. Untreated and IgG1 show low secretion. Amivantamab and Cetuximab show high secretion. For both cell lines, Amivantamab shows higher secretion than Cetuximab. A highly significant difference (**) is noted between Amivantamab and Cetuximab secretion.
L1807: Figure 14: Line graph showing Tumor Volume (mm^3) versus Days Post Tumor Implant (20 to 60). The control group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 1500 mm^3 by Day 60. The JNJ-61186372 (amivantamab) group shows significant inhibition of tumor growth, remaining below 500 mm^3. Erlotinib, osimertinib, and cetuximab groups show minimal or no effect on tumor growth compared to the control group. Asterisks (*) indicate statistical significance between the control group and the JNJ-61186372 group.
L1808: Figure 15: Panel A shows Tumor Volume (mm^3) versus Days on Study (0 to 40). Panel B shows Percent Survival versus Days on Study (0 to 60). Panel A indicates a Dosing phase (Days 0 to 14). In Panel A, PBS (control) shows rapid tumor growth. JNJ-61186372 (amivantamab) and Cetuximab show significant Tumor Growth Inhibition (TGI). Erlotinib and Osimertinib show minimal effect. In Panel B, PBS shows 100% survival until Day 15.5, then drops sharply. JNJ-61186372 and Cetuximab significantly increase survival, reaching approximately 44 and 31 days respectively. Erlotinib and Osimertinib show minimal effect on survival.
L1809: Figure 16 displays data related to amivantamab treatment in mice bearing PDCs with exon 20 insertion mutations.
D (DFCI-127, P772_H773insPNP): Line graph showing Tumor volume (\text{mm}^3) versus Day after treatment (1 to 11). Vehicle control (black line) shows increasing tumor volume, while Amivantamab 30 mg/kg (red line) shows significantly reduced tumor volume.
E (DFCI-127): Bar chart showing % change in tumor volume. Vehicle control shows high tumor volume (approx. 1000%), while Amivantamab 30 mg/kg shows a significant reduction (approx. 300%).
H (DFCI-127, P772_H773insPNP): Western blot analysis comparing Vehicle and Amivantamab 30 mg/kg treatment. Blots show pEGFR, EGFR, pMET, MET, and GAPDH. Amivantamab treatment significantly reduces the expression of pEGFR, EGFR, pMET, and MET.
F (YU-1163, S768_D770dup): Line graph showing Tumor volume (\text{mm}^3) versus Day after treatment (1 to 15). Vehicle control (black line) shows increasing tumor volume, while Amivantamab 30 mg/kg (red line) shows significantly reduced tumor volume.
G (YU-1163): Bar chart showing % change in tumor volume. Vehicle control shows high tumor volume (approx. 400%), while Amivantamab 30 mg/kg shows a significant reduction (approx. 100%).
I (YU-1163, S768_D770dup): Western blot analysis comparing Vehicle and Amivantamab 30 mg/kg treatment. Blots show pEGFR, EGFR, pMET, MET, and GAPDH. Amivantamab treatment significantly reduces the expression of pEGFR, EGFR, pMET, and MET.
L1810: Figure 17 is a graph showing Tumor volume (\text{mm}^3) versus Day after treatment (1 to 31) for YHIM-1029 (D770_N771insG) mice. The graph compares Vehicle, Amivantamab 10 mg/kg, Cetuximab 10 mg/kg, and Poziotinib 1 mg/kg. Amivantamab significantly reduces tumor volume compared to Vehicle and Poziotinib. The graph also includes a Western blot analysis showing the effect of Amivantamab (10 mg/kg) versus Vehicle on downstream effectors (pEGFR, EGFR, pMET, MET, pAKT, AKT, pERK, ERK, pS6, S6, p21, p27, p53, Cleaved caspase-3, Cleaved PARP, BIM, and GAPDH) in YHIM-1029 mice. Amivantamab treatment inhibits the phosphorylation of EGFR, MET, AKT, and ERK, and increases the levels of Cleaved caspase-3 and Cleaved PARP, indicating apoptosis induction.

L1816: Scatter plot showing Peak Total Bilirubin (Peak TB, Y-axis, logarithmic scale) versus Peak Maximum of ALT or AST (Peak AT, X-axis, logarithmic scale) for the All Treated Analysis Set in Monotherapy (Study 61186372EDI1001).
The Y-axis (Peak TB (xULN)) ranges from 0.1 to 100. The X-axis (Peak AT (xULN)) ranges from 0.1 to 100.
Key thresholds are marked:
2x (2 times ULN) on the Y-axis.
3x (3 times ULN) on the X-axis.
Hyperbilirubinemia (corresponding to Peak TB > 2x ULN).
Hy's Law range (corresponding to Peak TB > 2x ULN and Peak AT > 3x ULN).
Temple's Corollary range (corresponding to Peak TB > 2x ULN and Peak AT > 3x ULN).
The plot shows a large cluster of data points (Total) centered around 1x ULN for both Peak TB and Peak AT. A few outliers are visible, mostly below the 2x ULN threshold for Peak TB.

L1824: Figure 32 displays two plots showing the relationship between ORR (Objective Response Rate) and amivantamab exposure metrics in subjects with EGFR Exon 20 Insertion Mutation NSCLC. The left plot shows ORR versus C_{\text{trough.max}} (Serum JNJ61186372 Cmax concentration, \mu\text{g/mL}), and the right plot shows ORR versus C_{\text{trough.1st}} (Serum JNJ61186372 C1st concentration, \mu\text{g/mL}). Both plots show a fitted curve (line) representing the model fit, solid points representing observed ORR and associated 95% CI, and open circles representing predicted individual amivantamab C_{\text{trough.max}} corresponding to nonresponse (0) and response (1). Below the plots are horizontal boxplots summarizing C_{\text{trough.max}} for three dosing groups: Non-RP2D 1400 mg (<80 \text{ kg BW}), RP2D 1400 mg (\ge 80 \text{ kg BW}), and RP2D 1050 mg (<80 \text{ kg BW}). The ORR values for these groups are listed below the boxplots: Non-RP2D 1400 mg (<80 \text{ kg BW}) ORR: 42.9%; RP2D 1400 mg (\ge 80 \text{ kg BW}) ORR: 45.5%; RP2D 1050 mg (<80 \text{ kg BW}) ORR: 34.1%.
